by first building the clusters connected to
\nu
. These clusters consist of all vertices in
\overrightarrow{\mathcal{G}}
having a directed path in
\overrightarrow{\mathcal{G}}
to a point in
\nu
. When all such vertices have been added, we start a new cluster at the
\iota
-smallest point not yet added to
\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{cl}}
, say
{\bm{x}}_{k}
. This
{\bm{x}}_{k}
has no directed path to a
\iota
-smaller point in
\varphi
. The cluster started at
{\bm{x}}_{k}
consists of all vertices in
\overrightarrow{\mathcal{G}}
having a directed path towards
{\bm{x}}_{k}
, but not to any
\iota
-smaller point in
\varphi
. The order in which the points of the cluster is considered for thinning is given by the graph distance to
{\bm{x}}_{k}
(i.e. the minimal number edges in a directed path to
{\bm{x}}_{k}
). Two points with the same graph distance are ordered by
\iota
.
When all points of this cluster have been explored, we start a new cluster from the
\iota
-smallest point in
\varphi
not yet considered and so on.

Proof of Theorem
6.10
.

Let

T^{k}:=\xi_{1}\cup\dots\cup\xi_{k-1}\cup T^{\mathsf{RCM}}_{Q_{({\bm{x}}_{k},\infty)},\nu_{k},B_{k}}(\Phi^{*}_{Q_{({\bm{x}}_{k},\infty)},\nu\cup\psi}\setminus\bar{S}_{k-1}^{*}).

We show by induction that
T^{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\mathcal{X}(Q,\psi)
. Note that
T^{1}=T^{\mathsf{RCM}}_{Q_{({\bm{x}}_{1},\infty)},\nu_{1},\psi}(\Phi^{*}_{Q,\nu\cup\psi})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\mathcal{X}(Q,\psi)
by Lemma
6.5
.

Assume that the statement holds for
T^{k}
. Recall from the proof of Proposition
6.7
that
\mathcal{P}_{Q_{({\bm{x}}_{k},\infty)}}\setminus S_{k-1}^{*}
is a Poisson process of intensity
\rho_{k-1}\lambda
on
Q_{({\bm{x}}_{k},\infty)}
. Given
(\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{dc}}_{k})^{*}
with
V_{k}\neq\varnothing
, we have by Lemma
6.5
that

T^{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\xi_{1}\cup\dots\cup\xi_{k}\cup\mathcal{X}^{\rho_{k}\lambda}(Q_{({\bm{x}}_{k},\infty)},\xi_{k}\cup B_{k}\setminus\nu_{k})=\xi_{1}\cup\dots\cup\xi_{k}\cup\mathcal{X}^{\rho_{k}\lambda}(Q_{({\bm{x}}_{k+1},\infty)},B_{k+1}).

If
V_{k}=\varnothing
,
T^{\mathsf{RCM}}_{Q_{({\bm{x}}_{k},\infty)},\nu_{k},B_{k}}
is a standard Poisson embedding in an RCM, and hence the same holds by (
83
). By Lemma
6.5
,
the thinning

T^{\mathsf{RCM}}_{Q_{({\bm{x}}_{k+1},\infty)},\nu_{k+1},B_{k+1}}(\Phi_{Q_{({\bm{x}}_{k+1},\infty),\psi\cup\nu}}^{*}\setminus\bar{S}_{k}^{*})
can be used to construct the process
\mathcal{X}^{\rho_{k}\lambda}(Q_{({\bm{x}}_{k+1},\infty)},B_{k+1})
. Thus, still conditionally on
(\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{dc}}_{k})^{*}
,

T^{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\xi_{1}\cup\dots\cup\xi_{k}\cup T^{\mathsf{RCM}}_{Q_{({\bm{x}}_{k+1},\infty)},\nu_{k+1},B_{k+1}}(\Phi_{Q_{({\bm{x}}_{k+1},\infty)}\setminus S_{k}}^{*})=T^{k+1}.

Since this holds for any
(\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{dc}}_{k})^{*}
, it also holds unconditionally,
so
T^{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\mathcal{X}(Q,\psi)
implies
T^{k+1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\mathcal{X}(Q,\psi)
. Letting
k\to\infty
, we find
T^{\mathsf{RCM},\mathsf{dc}}_{Q,\nu,\psi}(\Phi^{*})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\mathcal{X}(Q,\psi)
.
∎

12.4.
Proofs for Section
6.3

To construct
\bar{T}^{\mathsf{RCM,cl}}_{Q,\nu,\psi}
, we again use an approximation on
Q^{\delta}
.
We introduce the PI on
\mathbb{X}^{\delta}
defined for
({\bm{x}},{\bm{r}})\in\mathbb{X}^{\delta}
and
\varphi^{\delta}\in\mathbf{N}_{\mathbb{X}^{\delta}}
by

\bar{\kappa}^{\delta}(({\bm{x}},{\bm{r}}),\varphi^{\delta})=\bar{\alpha}({\bm{x}})\mathbbmss{1}\{({\bm{x}},{\bm{r}})\not\approx_{\delta}\varphi^{\delta}\},

where we used the symmetric relation
({\bm{x}},{\bm{r}})\approx_{\delta}({\bm{y}},{\bm{s}})
if and only if
R^{\delta}(({\bm{x}},{\bm{r}}),({\bm{y}},{\bm{s}}))\leqslant\bar{\pi}({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})
, with
\bar{\pi}({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})=1-e^{-\bar{v}({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})}
. In particular,
({\bm{x}},{\bm{r}})\approx_{\delta}({\bm{y}},{\bm{s}})
implies
({\bm{x}},{\bm{r}})\sim_{\delta}({\bm{y}},{\bm{s}})
, where
\sim_{\delta}
is the relation given by
R^{\delta}(({\bm{x}},{\bm{r}}),({\bm{y}},{\bm{s}}))\leqslant{\pi}({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})
. This means that
\bar{\kappa}^{\delta}
satisfies (
6
) with the symmetric relation
\sim_{\delta}
. One can then define a thinning from fixed points by following the same steps as in the proof of Lemma
6.5
by setting
V_{\delta}=\{({\bm{x}},{\bm{r}})\mid({\bm{x}},{\bm{r}})\sim_{\delta}\nu^{\delta,0}\}
. We again use
V_{\delta}
to define a new ordering
\iota_{\delta}
(which agrees with the old one on
V_{\delta}
). This new ordering
\iota_{\delta}
defines a relation
\approx^{\delta}
which can be used in (
12.4
) to get a new PI
\bar{\kappa}_{\delta}
. We now use
\bar{\kappa}_{\delta}
and
\iota_{\delta}
in a Poisson embedding
T_{Q^{\delta},\nu^{\delta,0},\psi^{\delta,0}}(\Phi_{Q,\psi\cup\nu}^{*,\delta})
on
Q^{\delta}
. One can show as in Lemma
6.5
that when projecting to
Q
and letting
\delta\to 0
one obtains a copy of
\mathcal{X}(Q,\psi)
. We keep the part projected from
V_{\delta}
(i.e. the thinning of the points in
\Gamma_{\nu}(\Phi_{Q,\nu\cup\psi}^{*},\nu\cup\psi,\pi)
connected to
\nu
) and iterate the construction as in Definition
6.9
following a cluster-based exploration of
\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{cl}}(\Phi_{Q,\nu\cup\psi}^{*},\nu\cup\psi,\pi)
. We denote the resulting point process by
\bar{T}^{\mathsf{RCM,cl}}_{Q,\nu,\psi}\big(\Phi_{Q,\psi\cup\nu}^{*}\big)
. One may show as in Theorem
6.10
, that the result is a point process with PI
\bar{\kappa}
. We will not give the explicit formulas for retention probabilities, but they are very similar to (
40
) and (
42
), only one has to be careful about which of the relations
\sim
and
\approx
are used where and how the activity shows up.

Proof of Theorem
6.13
.

Let
\mathcal{X}_{i}
and
\mathcal{X}_{i}^{\prime}
denote the part of the processes generated in the
i
th step of the algorithm and let
\mathcal{X}_{i}^{\delta}
and
\mathcal{X}_{i}^{\prime,\delta}
denote the corresponding part of the processes generated on
Q^{\delta}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{X}_{i}\cap A\neq\mathcal{X}_{i}^{\prime}\cap A,\mathcal{X}_{j}\cap A=\mathcal{X}_{j}^{\prime}\cap A,j<i,E_{\mathsf{perc}}^{\mathsf{RCM}}(A,s)^{c}\cup E_{\mathsf{dens}}^{c}\mid\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{cl}}_{i-1}(\Phi_{Q,\varnothing}^{*})^{*})

\displaystyle\leqslant\lim_{\delta\to 0}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{X}_{i}^{\delta}\cap A^{\delta}\neq\mathcal{X}_{i}^{\prime,\delta}\cap A^{\delta},\mathcal{X}_{j}\cap A=\mathcal{X}_{j}^{\prime}\cap A,j<i,E_{\mathsf{perc}}^{\mathsf{RCM}}(A^{\delta},s)^{c}\cup E_{\mathsf{dens}}^{c}\mid\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{cl}}_{i-1}(\Phi_{Q,\varnothing}^{*})^{*}).

One may argue as in the proof of Theorem
5.9
to see that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{X}_{i}^{\delta}\cap A^{\delta}\neq\mathcal{X}_{i}^{\prime,\delta}\cap A^{\delta},\mathcal{X}_{j}\cap A=\mathcal{X}_{j}^{\prime}\cap A,j<i,E_{\mathsf{perc}}^{\mathsf{RCM}}(A^{\delta},s)^{c}\cup E_{\mathsf{dens}}^{c}\mid\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{cl}}_{i-1}(\Phi_{Q,\varnothing}^{*})^{*})

\displaystyle\leqslant 4\mathbb{E}\int_{B_{2s}(A^{\delta})}\mathbb{P}\Big(({\bm{x}},{\bm{r}})\leftrightsquigarrow_{\{({\bm{x}},{\bm{r}})\}\cup\Phi_{Q\setminus S_{i-1},\bar{\eta}}^{\delta,*}\cup\bar{\eta}^{\delta}}(\Phi_{Q\setminus S_{i-1},\bar{\eta}}^{*,\delta}\cap(Q^{\delta}\setminus P^{\delta}))\cup\bar{\eta}^{\delta}\Big)(\lambda\otimes\mathsf{Leb}_{[0,1]}^{\delta})({\rm d}({\bm{x}},{\bm{r}}))

\displaystyle+2\mathbb{E}\int_{Q^{\delta}\setminus B_{2s}(A^{\delta})}\int_{B_{s}(A^{\delta})}\mathbb{P}(({\bm{x}},{\bm{r}})\sim^{\delta}({\bm{y}},{\bm{r}}))(\lambda\otimes\mathsf{Leb}_{[0,1]}^{\delta})({\rm d}({\bm{x}},{\bm{r}}))(\lambda\otimes\mathsf{Leb}_{[0,1]}^{\delta})({\rm d}({\bm{y}},{\bm{s}})).

Here
\Phi_{Q\setminus S_{i-1},\bar{\eta}}^{*,\delta}
is the unexplored process before step
i
. Letting
\delta\to 0
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{X}_{i}\cap A\neq\mathcal{X}_{i}^{\prime}\cap A,\mathcal{X}_{j}\cap A=\mathcal{X}_{j}^{\prime}\cap A,j<i,E_{\mathsf{perc}}^{\mathsf{RCM}}(A,s)^{c}\cup E_{\mathsf{dens}}^{c}\mid\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{cl}}_{i-1}(\Phi_{Q,\varnothing}^{*})^{*})

\displaystyle\leqslant 4\mathbb{E}\int_{B_{2s}(A)}\mathbb{P}\Big({\bm{x}}\leftrightsquigarrow_{\Gamma(Q,\bar{\eta})}\mathcal{P}_{Q\setminus P}\cup\bar{\eta}\Big)\lambda({\rm d}{\bm{x}})+2\mathbb{E}\int_{Q\setminus B_{2s}(A)}\int_{B_{s}(A)}\pi({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})\lambda({\rm d}{\bm{x}})\lambda({\rm d}{\bm{y}}).

Summing the above and using that
i\leqslant M=4\lambda(Q)
under
E_{\mathsf{dens}}
, we obtain the claim.
∎

Proof of Lemma
6.14
.

We may write

\displaystyle\kappa^{\prime}({\bm{x}},\varphi)

\displaystyle=\mathbbmss{1}\{{\bm{x}}\in P_{1}\setminus P_{3}\}e^{-\sum_{{\bm{y}}\in\psi_{1}}v({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})}e^{-\sum_{{\bm{y}}\in\varphi}v({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})\mathbbmss{1}\{{\bm{y}}\in P_{1}\setminus P_{3}\}}

which has the form (
44
) with
\bar{\alpha}({\bm{x}})=e^{-\sum_{{\bm{y}}\in\psi_{1}}v({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})}\mathbbmss{1}\{{\bm{x}}\in P_{1}\}
and
\bar{v}({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})=v({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})\mathbbmss{1}\{{\bm{x}},{\bm{y}}\in P_{1}\}
.
Moreover,

\displaystyle\kappa^{\prime\prime}({\bm{x}},\varphi)=e^{-\sum_{{\bm{y}}\in\psi_{1}}v({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})}\mathbbmss{1}\{{\bm{x}}\in P_{1,-}\setminus P_{3}\}e^{-\sum_{{\bm{y}}\in\varphi}v({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})\mathbbmss{1}\{{\bm{x}},{\bm{y}}\in P_{1,-}\setminus P_{3}\}}

\displaystyle+e^{-\sum_{{\bm{y}}\in\psi_{2}}v({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})}\mathbbmss{1}\{{\bm{x}}\in P_{2,-}\}e^{-\sum_{{\bm{y}}\in\varphi}v({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})\mathbbmss{1}\{{\bm{x}},{\bm{y}}\in P_{2,-}\}}

\displaystyle=\Big(e^{-\sum_{{\bm{y}}\in\psi_{1}}v({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})}\mathbbmss{1}\{{\bm{x}}\in P_{1,-}\setminus P_{3}\}+e^{-\sum_{{\bm{y}}\in\psi_{2}}v({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})}\mathbbmss{1}\{{\bm{x}}\in P_{2,-}\}\Big)e^{-\sum_{{\bm{y}}\in\varphi}v({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})\mathbbmss{1}\{{\bm{x}},{\bm{y}}\in P_{1,-}\setminus P_{3}\text{ or }{\bm{x}},{\bm{y}}\in P_{2,-}\}},

which again has form (
44
). By construction
\kappa^{\prime}
and
\kappa^{\prime\prime}
satisfy (
45
) with
\alpha({\bm{x}})=\mathbbmss{1}\{{\bm{x}}\notin P_{3}\}
.
∎

12.5.
Proofs for Section
6.4

Proof of Lemma
6.15
.

In the following, the intersection of a graph
\mathcal{G}
with a set
A
means intersecting the vertex set with
A
and taking all induced edges. For the proof, we consider the directed graph
\overrightarrow{\mathcal{G}}:=\overrightarrow{\mathcal{G}}(\Phi^{*}_{U,\psi},\psi)
with vertex set
\mathcal{P}_{U}\cup\psi
and directed edges
{\bm{x}}\to{\bm{y}}
if
r_{{\bm{x}},{\bm{y}}}\leqslant\pi({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})
and
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{P}_{U}
.

Recall the construction of the random graphs
\mathcal{G}^{n}:=\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{cl}}(\Phi^{*}_{U_{n},\psi\cup\nu_{n}},\psi)
and
\overrightarrow{\mathcal{G}}^{n}:=\overrightarrow{\mathcal{G}}(\Phi^{*}_{U_{n},\psi\cup\nu_{n}},\psi\cup\nu_{n})
from Definition
6.6
and Remark
12.3
. Let
n^{\prime}>n
. Then
\overrightarrow{\mathcal{G}}^{n}\subseteq\overrightarrow{\mathcal{G}}^{n^{\prime}}
and
\overrightarrow{\mathcal{G}}^{n}\cap U_{n}=\overrightarrow{\mathcal{G}}^{n^{\prime}}\cap U_{n}
. A connected component
\mathcal{C}^{n}
in
\mathcal{G}^{n}
that is not connected to
\nu_{n}
is started from a point
{\bm{x}}_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{U_{n}}
that has no directed path in
\overrightarrow{\mathcal{G}}^{n}
to a
\iota
-smaller point (we ignore edges to
\psi
in the following because these coincide with edges to
\psi
in
\overrightarrow{\mathcal{G}}
for all
\mathcal{G}^{n}
). Then, there is also no directed path from
{\bm{x}}_{0}
to an
\iota
-smaller point in
\overrightarrow{\mathcal{G}}^{n^{\prime}}
since
\overrightarrow{\mathcal{G}}^{n}\cap U_{n}=\overrightarrow{\mathcal{G}}^{n^{\prime}}\cap U_{n}
implies that such a path would have to exit
U_{n}
at some point, meaning that
{\bm{x}}_{0}
would be connected to a point in
\nu_{n}
in
\mathcal{G}^{n}
, which is a contradiction. Thus,
{\bm{x}}_{0}
is also a starting point of a cluster
\mathcal{C}^{n^{\prime}}
in
\mathcal{G}^{n^{\prime}}
. The cluster
\mathcal{C}^{n}
consists of all
{\bm{x}}>_{\iota}{\bm{x}}_{0}
having a directed path in
\overrightarrow{\mathcal{G}}^{n}
to
{\bm{x}}_{0}
and not to any
\iota
-smaller point. This path would also be a directed path from
{\bm{x}}
to
{\bm{x}}_{0}
in
\overrightarrow{\mathcal{G}}^{n^{\prime}}
. If
{\bm{x}}
had a path to an
\iota
-smaller point in
\overrightarrow{\mathcal{G}}^{n^{\prime}}
, the part of this path until its first exit of
U_{n}
would again form a path from
{\bm{x}}
to
\nu_{n}
in
\mathcal{G}^{n}
, which is a contradiction. Hence
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{C}^{n^{\prime}}
. On the other hand, if
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{C}^{n^{\prime}}
, then it has a directed path to
{\bm{x}}_{0}
and not to any smaller point. This path would lie in
\mathcal{G}^{n}
since otherwise a subpath would connect
{\bm{x}}
to a smaller point than
{\bm{x}}_{0}
. This shows
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{C}^{n}
. In total, we have shown
\mathcal{C}^{n}=\mathcal{C}^{n^{\prime}}
. This implies that
\mathcal{G}^{n}
and
\mathcal{G}^{n^{\prime}}
agree on all connected components of
\mathcal{G}^{n}
not connected to
\nu_{n}
and thus, we may define
\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{cl}}(\Phi^{*}_{U,\psi},\psi)
as in Lemma
6.15
.

We need to see that
\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{cl}}(\Phi^{*}_{U,\psi},\psi)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\Gamma(U,\psi)
. By Theorem
6.10
, each
\mathcal{G}^{n}
is an RCM
\Gamma(U_{n},\psi)
. This can be extended to an RCM
\Gamma^{n}(U,\psi)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\Gamma(U,\psi)
on
\mathbb{X}
by adding edges independently between points in
\nu_{n}
and from
\nu_{n}
to
\psi
. Then, for any
W_{n}
and
m>n
,

\mathbb{P}\big(\Gamma^{m}(U,\psi)\cap W_{n}\neq\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{cl}}(\Phi^{*}_{U,\psi},\psi)\cap W_{n}\big)\leqslant\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{P}_{U_{n}}\leftrightsquigarrow_{\Gamma^{m}(U,\psi)}\nu_{m}\big).

Each
\Gamma^{m}(U,\psi)
is an RCM so

\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{P}_{U_{n}}\leftrightsquigarrow_{\Gamma^{m}(U,\psi)}\nu_{m}\big)\leqslant\mathbb{E}\sum_{{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{P}_{U_{n}}}\mathbbmss{1}\{{\bm{x}}\leftrightsquigarrow_{\Gamma_{{\bm{x}}}(U,\psi)}\mathcal{P}_{U\setminus U_{m}}\}=\int_{U_{n}}\mathbb{P}({\bm{x}}\leftrightsquigarrow_{\Gamma_{{\bm{x}}}(U,\psi)}\mathcal{P}_{U\setminus U_{m}})\lambda(\,{\rm d}{\bm{x}})

tends to 0 as
m\to\infty
by assumption (
15
). Hence,
\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{cl}}(\Phi^{*}_{U,\psi},\psi)
has the same distribution as each
\Gamma^{m}(U,\psi)
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
6.16
.

Suppose we are on the event
\{\mathcal{P}_{A\cap U}\not\leftrightsquigarrow_{\Gamma}\nu_{n}\}
.
Let
m\geqslant n
and
{\bm{x}}_{0}
be the
\iota
-smallest point in the clusters connected to
A
. By Remark
12.3
,
T^{\mathsf{RCM,\infty}}_{U_{m},\nu_{m},\psi}
will first consider all clusters of
\mathcal{G}^{m}=\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{cl}}(\Phi^{*}_{U_{m},\psi\cup\nu_{m}},\psi)
having a point
\iota
-smaller than
{\bm{x}}_{0}
. From there, the points will be visited in the same order for any
m
.
Moreover, the retention probabilities
p^{\mathsf{RCM}}({\bm{x}},Q_{k},\nu_{k},B_{k},\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{cl}}_{k})
will only depend on the clusters with no
\iota
-smaller point than
{\bm{x}}_{0}
, which are the same for all
m
.

The limiting Gibbs distribution is first verified for
T^{\mathsf{RCM,\infty}}_{\mathbb{X},\varnothing}(\Phi^{*}_{\mathbb{X},\varnothing})
by checking the GNZ-equation for bounded
f
of the form
f({\bm{x}},\varphi)=f({\bm{x}},\varphi\cap W_{s})\mathbbmss{1}\{{\bm{x}}\in W_{s}\}
and then for
\Psi\cup T^{\mathsf{RCM,\infty}}_{U,\Psi}(\Phi^{*}_{U,\Psi})
as in Proposition
5.11
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
6.18
.

To show (
48
), let
\mathcal{X}_{m}=T^{\mathsf{RCM,cl},2}_{U_{m},\mu,\nu_{m},\psi\cup\mu_{i}}(\Phi^{*}_{U_{m},\psi\cup\mu\cup\nu_{m}})
and let
n+1<m<m^{\prime}
. Then,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathcal{X}_{m}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mu}\neq\mathcal{X}_{m^{\prime}}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mu},\,\mathcal{C}_{\mu}\subseteq W_{n}\Big)

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{k\geqslant 1}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{X}_{m}\cap V_{k}\neq\mathcal{X}_{m^{\prime}}\cap V_{k},\mathcal{X}_{m}\cap S_{k-1}=\mathcal{X}_{m^{\prime}}\cap S_{k-1},S_{k}\subseteq W_{n},k\leqslant k_{0})

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{k\geqslant 1}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbbmss{1}\{\mathcal{X}_{m}\cap S_{k-1}=\mathcal{X}_{m^{\prime}}\cap S_{k-1},k\leqslant k_{0}\}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{X}_{m}\cap V_{k}\neq\mathcal{X}_{m^{\prime}}\cap V_{k},S_{k}\subseteq W_{n}\mid(\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{cl},2}_{k-1})^{*})\bigg],

where
k_{0}
is the (random) number of steps it takes to explore
\mathcal{C}_{\mu}
.
As long as we are only thinning
\mathcal{C}_{\mu}
, it does not matter in which order we explore the later clusters. Hence,

\mathcal{X}_{m}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mu}=T^{\mathsf{RCM,cl},2}_{U_{m},\mu,\nu_{m},\psi\cup\mu_{i}}(\Phi^{*}_{U_{m},\psi\cup\mu\cup\nu_{m}})\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mu}=T^{\mathsf{RCM,cl}}_{U_{m},\mu,\psi\cup\mu_{i}}(\Phi^{*}_{U_{m},\psi\cup\mu\cup\nu_{m}})\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mu},

and a similar statement holds for
m^{\prime}
.
Note that
\mathcal{X}(U_{m},\psi\cup\mu_{i})

can be considered as a Gibbs process
\bar{\mathcal{X}}(U_{m^{\prime}},\psi\cup\mu_{i})
on
U_{m^{\prime}}
with a perturbed PI of the form

\bar{\kappa}({\bm{x}},\varphi)=\kappa\big({\bm{x}},\varphi\setminus(U_{m^{\prime}}\setminus U_{m})\big)\mathbbmss{1}\{{\bm{x}}\in U_{m}\}.

Construct
\bar{\mathcal{X}}(U_{m^{\prime}},\psi\cup\mu_{i})
by a thinning
\bar{T}^{\mathsf{RCM,cl}}_{U_{m^{\prime}},\mu,\psi\cup\mu_{i}}
as explained in Section
6.3
. When
\mathcal{C}_{\mu}\subseteq W_{n}
,

\mathcal{X}_{m}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mu}=T^{\mathsf{RCM,cl}}_{U_{m},\mu,\psi\cup\mu_{i}}(\Phi^{*}_{U_{m},\psi\cup\mu\cup\nu_{m}})\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mu}=\bar{T}^{\mathsf{RCM,cl}}_{U_{m^{\prime}},\mu,\psi\cup\mu_{i}}(\Phi^{*}_{U_{m^{\prime}},\psi\cup\mu\cup\nu_{m^{\prime}}})\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mu}

since the points of
\mathcal{C}_{\mu}
are visited in the same order by
T^{\mathsf{RCM,cl}}_{U_{m},\mu,\psi\cup\mu_{i}}
and
\bar{T}^{\mathsf{RCM,cl}}_{U_{m^{\prime}},\mu,\psi\cup\mu_{i}}
and the retention probabilities using
\kappa^{\prime}
are the same (indeed, in the formulas for the retention probabilities (
40
) and (
42
), the partition functions in the numerator and denominator are multiplied by the same constant, namely the probability that the relevant Poisson process has no points in
U_{m^{\prime}}\setminus U_{m}
). Set
\bar{\mathcal{X}}_{m}=\bar{T}^{\mathsf{RCM,cl}}_{U_{m^{\prime}},\mu,\psi\cup\mu_{i}}(\Phi^{*}_{U_{m^{\prime}},\psi\cup\mu\cup\nu_{m^{\prime}}})
.
Then,

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geqslant 1}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbbmss{1}\{\mathcal{X}_{m}\cap S_{k-1}=\mathcal{X}_{m^{\prime}}\cap S_{k-1},k\leqslant k_{0}\}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{X}_{m}\cap V_{k}\neq\mathcal{X}_{m^{\prime}}\cap V_{k},S_{k}\subseteq W_{n},\mid(\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{cl},2}_{k-1})^{*})\big]

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{k\geqslant 1}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbbmss{1}\{\bar{\mathcal{X}}_{m}\cap S_{k-1}=\mathcal{X}_{m^{\prime}}\cap S_{k-1},k\leqslant k_{0},S_{k-1}\subseteq W_{n}\}\mathbb{P}(\bar{\mathcal{X}}_{m}\cap V_{k}\neq{\mathcal{X}}_{m^{\prime}}\cap V_{k}\mid(\mathcal{G}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{cl}})^{*})\big]

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{k\geqslant 1}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbbmss{1}\{k\leqslant k_{0},S_{k-1}\subseteq W_{n}\}

(89)

\displaystyle\quad\times\mathbb{E}\bigg[2\int_{U_{m^{\prime}}}\mathbbmss{1}\{{\bm{x}}\sim_{\Gamma_{k-1,{\bm{x}}}}V_{k-1}\cap W_{n}\}\mathbbmss{1}\{{\bm{x}}\leftrightsquigarrow_{\Gamma_{k-1,{\bm{x}}}}\nu_{m}\}\lambda({\rm d}{\bm{x}})\mid(\mathcal{G}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{cl}})^{*}\bigg]\bigg],

where
\Gamma_{k-1,{\bm{x}}}
denotes the graph
\Gamma_{k-1}
from (
87
) under the Palm distribution at
{\bm{x}}
of the underlying Poisson process.
To see where the second inequality came from, we consider the
\delta
-approximations as in the proof of Theorem
6.13
. Let
V_{1}^{\delta}=\{({\bm{x}},{\bm{r}})\in U_{m^{\prime}}^{\delta}:({\bm{x}},{\bm{r}})\sim_{\delta}\mu\}
and
V_{i}^{\delta}=\{({\bm{x}},{\bm{r}})\in U_{m^{\prime}}^{\delta}:({\bm{x}},{\bm{r}})\sim_{\delta}V_{i-1}^{\delta}\}
and let
\mathcal{X}^{\delta}_{m,i}
and
\bar{\mathcal{X}}_{m,i}^{\delta}
be the part of the Gibbs processes constructed in step
i
. Conditionally on the points explored up to step
k-1
, i.e.
\Phi_{U_{m^{\prime}},\psi\cup\mu}^{*}\cap S_{k-1}
, if the thinnings agreed so far, we have from Corollary
5.8
that

\displaystyle\mathbbmss{1}\{\mathcal{X}_{m,i}\neq\bar{\mathcal{X}}_{m,i},i\leqslant k-1\}\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{X}_{k}^{\delta}\neq\bar{\mathcal{X}}_{k}^{\delta}\mid\Phi_{U_{m^{\prime}},\psi\cup\mu}^{*}\cap S_{k-1}\big)

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\int_{V_{k}^{\delta}}\mathbb{P}\Big({\bm{x}}\leftrightsquigarrow_{\{{\bm{x}}\}\cup\Phi_{U_{m^{\prime}},\psi\cup\mu}^{*,\delta}}\Phi_{U_{m^{\prime}},\psi\cup\mu}^{*,\delta}\cap(U^{\delta}_{m^{\prime}}\setminus U_{m}^{\delta})\Big)\big(\lambda\otimes\mathsf{Leb}_{[0,1]}^{\delta}\big)({\rm d}{\bm{x}}).

Letting
\delta\to 0
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbbmss{1}\{\bar{\mathcal{X}}_{m}\cap S_{k-1}=\mathcal{X}_{m^{\prime}}\cap S_{k-1}\}\mathbb{P}(\bar{\mathcal{X}}_{m}\cap V_{k}\neq{\mathcal{X}}_{m^{\prime}}\cap V_{k}\mid(\mathcal{G}_{k-1}^{\mathsf{cl}})^{*})\leqslant 2\int_{U_{m^{\prime}}}\mathbb{P}({\bm{x}}\sim V_{k-1},{\bm{x}}\leftrightsquigarrow_{\Gamma_{k-1,{\bm{x}}}}\mathcal{P}_{U\setminus U_{m}})\lambda({\rm d}{\bm{x}}),

which is what was used in (
89
).

Note that only the conditional distribution of
\Gamma_{k-1}=\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{dc}}_{k-1}\cup\Gamma^{\mathsf{Init}}_{V_{k}}\cup\Gamma^{\mathsf{Bulk}}_{V_{k}}
given
(\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{cl}}_{k-1})^{*}
matters in (
89
), and this is the same as the distribution of
\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{cl},2}
.
Splitting the integral after whether
{\bm{x}}\in U_{m-1}
or
{\bm{x}}\in U_{m^{\prime}}\setminus U_{m-1}
, we obtain the bound on (
89
)

\displaystyle 2\sum_{k\geqslant 1}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbbmss{1}\{k\leqslant k_{0}\}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{U_{m^{\prime}}\setminus U_{m-1}}\mathbbmss{1}\{{\bm{x}}\notin S_{k-1,{\bm{x}}},{\bm{x}}\sim_{\mathcal{G}_{{\bm{x}}}^{\mathsf{cl},2}}V_{k-1,{\bm{x}}}\cap W_{n}\}\lambda({\rm d}{\bm{x}})\mid(\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{cl},2}_{k-1})^{*}\bigg]\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+2\sum_{k\geqslant 1}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbbmss{1}\{k\leqslant k_{0}\}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{U_{m-1}}\mathbbmss{1}\{{\bm{x}}\notin S_{k-1,{\bm{x}}},{\bm{x}}\sim_{\mathcal{G}_{{\bm{x}}}^{\mathsf{cl},2}}V_{k-1,{\bm{x}}}\}\mathbbmss{1}\{{\bm{x}}\leftrightsquigarrow_{\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{cl},2}_{{\bm{x}}}}\nu_{m}\}\lambda({\rm d}{\bm{x}})\mid(\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{cl},2}_{k-1})^{*}\bigg]\bigg]

\displaystyle=2\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{k=1}^{k_{0}}\int_{U_{m^{\prime}}\setminus U_{m-1}}\mathbbmss{1}\{{\bm{x}}\notin S_{k-1,{\bm{x}}},{\bm{x}}\sim_{\mathcal{G}_{{\bm{x}}}^{\mathsf{cl},2}}V_{k-1,{\bm{x}}}\cap W_{n}\}\lambda({\rm d}{\bm{x}})\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+2\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{k=1}^{k_{0}}\int_{U_{m-1}}\mathbbmss{1}\{{\bm{x}}\notin S_{k-1,{\bm{x}}},{\bm{x}}\sim_{\mathcal{G}_{{\bm{x}}}^{\mathsf{cl},2}}V_{k-1,{\bm{x}}}\}\mathbbmss{1}\{{\bm{x}}\leftrightsquigarrow_{\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{cl},2}_{{\bm{x}}}}\nu_{m}\}\lambda({\rm d}{\bm{x}})\bigg]

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{U_{m^{\prime}}\setminus U_{m-1}}\mathbbmss{1}\{{\bm{x}}\sim_{\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{cl},2}_{{\bm{x}}}}\mu\cup\mathcal{P}_{W_{n}}\}\lambda({\rm d}{\bm{x}})\Big]+2\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{U_{m-1}}\mathbbmss{1}\{{\bm{x}}\leftrightsquigarrow_{\mathcal{G}_{{\bm{x}}}^{\mathsf{cl},2}}\nu_{m}\}\lambda({\rm d}{\bm{x}})\Big].

In the inequality, we used that the events
\{{\bm{x}}\notin S_{k-1,{\bm{x}}},{\bm{x}}\sim_{\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{cl},2}_{{\bm{x}}}}V_{k-1,{\bm{x}}}\cap W_{n}\}\subseteq\{{\bm{x}}\in V_{k,{\bm{x}}}\}
are disjoint. We further get the bound

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}\int_{U\setminus U_{m-1}}\sum_{{\bm{y}}\in\mu}\pi({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})\lambda({\rm d}{\bm{x}})+2\mathbb{E}\int_{U\setminus U_{m-1}}\sum_{{\bm{y}}\in\mathcal{P}_{W_{n}}}\pi({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})\lambda({\rm d}{\bm{y}})\lambda({\rm d}{\bm{x}})+2\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{U_{m-1}}\mathbbmss{1}\{{\bm{x}}\leftrightsquigarrow_{\Gamma_{{\bm{x}}}}\nu_{m}\}\lambda({\rm d}{\bm{x}})\Big]

\displaystyle\leqslant 2c\int_{U\setminus U_{m-1}}\int_{W_{1}}\pi({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})\lambda({\rm d}{\bm{y}})\lambda({\rm d}{\bm{x}})+2\int_{U\setminus U_{m-1}}\int_{W_{n}}\pi({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})\lambda({\rm d}{\bm{y}})\lambda({\rm d}{\bm{x}})+2\int_{U_{m-1}}\mathbb{P}({\bm{x}}\leftrightsquigarrow_{\Gamma_{{\bm{x}}}}\nu_{m})\lambda({\rm d}{\bm{x}}).

Taking
m=n+2
and
m^{\prime}=n+3
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{X}_{n+2}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mu}\neq\mathcal{X}_{n+3}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mu}\big)

\displaystyle\leqslant 2c\int_{\mathbb{X}\setminus W_{n+1}}\int_{W_{1}}\pi({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})\lambda({\rm d}{\bm{y}})\lambda({\rm d}{\bm{x}})+2\int_{\mathbb{X}\setminus W_{n+1}}\int_{W_{n}}\pi({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})\lambda({\rm d}{\bm{y}})\lambda({\rm d}{\bm{x}})

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{W_{n+1}}\mathbb{P}({\bm{x}}\leftrightsquigarrow_{\Gamma_{{\bm{x}}}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{X}\setminus W_{n+2}})\lambda({\rm d}{\bm{x}})+\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{C}_{\mu}\cap(\mathbb{X}\setminus W_{n})\neq\varnothing)

\displaystyle\leqslant 2c\cdot 2^{-n}+2\cdot 2^{-n}+2\cdot 2^{-n-1}+c\cdot 2^{-n+1}.

These probabilities are summable, hence by the Borel-Cantelli lemma, there is an
N(\omega)
s.t.
\mathcal{X}_{n}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mu}=\mathcal{X}_{N}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mu}
for all
n\geqslant N
. This shows (
48
).

To show (
49
), note that we first construct
\mathcal{C}_{\mu}
as the set of points in
\mathcal{P}_{U}
having a directed path to
\mu
in
\overrightarrow{\mathcal{G}}(\Psi_{U_{m},\mu\cup\psi\cup\nu_{m}})
. The clusters of the remaining points are constructed as in the proof of Lemma
6.15
. Hence, the argument that the thinnings agree outside
\mathcal{C}_{\mu}
from the proof of Proposition
6.16
carries over to show the second statement. Since the boundary conditions
\mu_{i}
only affect the thinning of
\mathcal{C}_{\mu}
, (
54
) follows immediately.

The limiting Gibbs distribution in (
51
) is verified by checking the GNZ equations for
\Psi\cup T^{\mathsf{RCM},\infty,2}_{U,\mu,\Psi}(\Phi^{*}_{U,\Psi\cup\mu})
, noting that
T^{\mathsf{RCM},\infty,2}_{U,\mu,\Psi}(\Phi^{*}_{U,\Psi\cup\mu})
equals
T^{\mathsf{RCM,cl},2}_{U_{m},\mu,\nu_{m},\Psi}(\Phi^{*}_{U_{m},\Psi\cup\mu\cup\nu_{m}})
for
m\geqslant M
for some
M(\omega)
.
∎

13.
Disagreement coupling proofs for Section
10

The main challenge in proving Lemma
10.6
is that when we modify the Poisson process we are thinning, then the unexplored part of the domain in a given step of the cluster-based thinning algorithm may look different. Thus, a central ingredient in the proof is the following lemma that bounds the effect of changing the domain.

Lemma 13.1
.

Consider a PI
\kappa
from Model (L) on a set
P
. Define two PIs
\kappa_{i}({\bm{x}},\varphi)=\kappa({\bm{x}},\varphi)\mathbbmss{1}\{{\bm{x}}\in P_{i}\}
where
P_{i}\subseteq P
,
i=1,2
. Denote the corresponding Poisson embeddings by
T^{i}_{P,\psi}(\mathcal{P}^{*}_{P})
. Let
M>0
.
Then,

\mathbb{P}(T^{1}_{P,\psi}(\mathcal{P}^{*}_{P})\neq T^{2}_{P,\psi}(\mathcal{P}^{*}_{P}))\leqslant M\big(2+\lambda(P)\big)\lambda(P_{1}\Delta P_{2})+\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{P}(P)>M).

Proof.

From Lemma
5.7
, we get that

\mathbb{P}(\inf_{\iota}T^{1}_{P,\psi}(\mathcal{P}^{*}_{P})\cap P\neq\inf_{\iota}T^{2}_{P,\psi}(\mathcal{P}^{*}_{P})\cap P)\leqslant\big(2+\lambda(P)\big)d_{\mathsf{TV}}(T^{1}_{P,\psi}(\mathcal{P}^{*}_{P})\cap P,T^{2}_{P,\psi}(\mathcal{P}^{*}_{P})\cap P).

We can construct Gibbs processes with the same distribution as
T^{1}_{P,\psi}(\mathcal{P}^{*}_{P})
and
T^{2}_{P,\psi}(\mathcal{P}^{*}_{P})
by a Poisson embedding using an ordering where
P_{1}\Delta P_{2}
comes before
P\setminus(P_{1}\Delta P_{2})
. If
\mathcal{P}_{P_{1}\Delta P_{2}}=\varnothing
, the PIs look the same on
P\setminus(P_{1}\Delta P_{2})
and have the same boundary conditions. Hence, the Poisson embeddings will agree. Thus,

\mathbb{P}\big(\inf_{\iota}T^{1}_{P,\psi}(\mathcal{P}^{*}_{P})\cap P\neq\inf_{\iota}T^{2}_{P,\psi}(\mathcal{P}^{*}_{P})\cap P\big)\leqslant\big(2+\lambda(P)\big)\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{P}_{P_{1}\Delta P_{2}}\neq\varnothing)\leqslant\big(2+\lambda(P)\big)\lambda(P_{1}\Delta P_{2}).

Using this argument repeatedly when
\mathcal{P}(P)\leqslant M
, we get

\mathbb{P}\big(T^{1}_{P,\psi}(\mathcal{P}^{*}_{P})\neq T^{2}_{P,\psi}(\mathcal{P}^{*}_{P})\big)\leqslant M\big(2+\lambda(P)\big)\lambda(P_{1}\Delta P_{2})+\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{P}(P)>M\big).

∎

Now, we have all the ingredients to prove Lemma
10.6
. Again,
C>0
denotes a constant which may change value from line to line. To shorten notation, we write
Q
for
Q_{n}^{+}
.

Proof of Lemma
10.6
.

We show the inequality for
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{X}_{{\bm{x}}}^{\prime\prime\prime}\neq\check{\mathcal{X}}_{{\bm{x}}}^{\prime\prime\prime})
. The remaining inequalities are similar. We first consider Model (L), which is conceptually simpler, and then sketch the generalization to Model (P).

Model (L).

Let

\mathcal{Y}=\{{\bm{w}}\in\mathcal{P}\cup\check{\mathcal{P}}\mid{\bm{w}}\leftrightsquigarrow_{\mathcal{P}}\mathcal{P}_{Q\setminus B_{2r}({\bm{x}})}\text{ or }{\bm{w}}\leftrightsquigarrow_{\check{\mathcal{P}}}\check{\mathcal{P}}_{Q\setminus B_{2r}({\bm{x}})}\}.

Recall that
\mathcal{P}
and
\check{\mathcal{P}}
agree on
B_{2r}({\bm{x}})
, while
\mathcal{P}\cup\check{\mathcal{P}}
forms a Poisson process of double intensity outside this ball. We refer to Figure
5
for an illustration.
The clusters of
\mathcal{P}\setminus\mathcal{Y}\subseteq B_{2r}({\bm{x}})
correspond to clusters in both
\mathcal{P}
and
\check{\mathcal{P}}
that do not contain a point in
Q\setminus B_{2r}({\bm{x}})
. The exploration algorithms for constructing
\mathcal{X}_{{\bm{x}}}^{\prime\prime\prime}
and
\check{\mathcal{X}}_{{\bm{x}}}^{\prime\prime\prime}
visit the points of
\mathcal{P}\setminus\mathcal{Y}=\check{\mathcal{P}}\setminus\mathcal{Y}
in the same order, but different points in
\mathcal{Y}
may be visited in between. If the thinning of
\mathcal{P}
explores points of
\mathcal{P}\setminus\mathcal{Y}
in step
k+1
, there is a
\check{k}\geqslant 1
such that the thinning of
\check{\mathcal{P}}
considers the same points in step
\check{k}+1
.
For this to happen, we must be under one of the following two events, which are illustrated in Figure
6
:

(1)

E_{1,k,\check{k}}=E_{1,k}^{(1)}\cap E_{1,k,\check{k}}^{(2)}\cap E_{1,k,\check{k}}^{(3)}
, where

\displaystyle E_{1,k}^{(1)}=

\displaystyle\big\{\text{in step }k+1\text{ of the exploration of }\mathcal{P},\text{ we continue exploring a cluster contained in }B_{2r}({\bm{x}})\};

\displaystyle E_{1,k,\check{k}}^{(2)}=

\displaystyle\big\{\text{in step }\check{k}+1\text{ of the exploration of }\check{\mathcal{P}},\text{ we continue exploring a cluster which up to step $\check{k}$ looks}

\displaystyle\text{the same as the one considered up to step $k$ in }\mathcal{P}\big\};

\displaystyle E_{1,k,\check{k}}^{(3)}=

\displaystyle\big\{\text{the points explored in the next step agree, i.e. }\mathcal{P}_{S_{k+1}\setminus S_{k}}=\check{\mathcal{P}}_{\check{S}_{\check{k}+1}\setminus\check{S}_{\check{k}}}\big\}.

(2)

E_{2,k,\check{k}}=E_{2,k}^{(1)}\cap E_{2,\check{k}}^{(2)}\cap E_{2,k,\check{k}}^{(3)}
, where

\displaystyle E_{2,k}^{(1)}=

\displaystyle\big\{\text{in step }k+1\text{ of the exploration of }\mathcal{P},\text{ we start a new cluster}\};

\displaystyle E_{2,\check{k}}^{(2)}=

\displaystyle\big\{\text{in step }\check{k}+1\text{ of the exploration of }\check{\mathcal{P}},\text{ we start a new cluster}\big\};

\displaystyle E_{2,k,\check{k}}^{(3)}=

\displaystyle\big\{\text{the smallest unexplored Poisson point of the exploration at step $k$ + 1 in }\mathcal{P}

\displaystyle\text{ and of the exploration at step }\check{k}+1\text{ in }\check{\mathcal{P}}\text{ agree}\big\}.

Q
B_{2r}({\bm{x}})
{\bm{x}}

Figure 5.

Coupled configurations
\mathcal{P}
and
\check{\mathcal{P}}
in the domain
Q
.
Black points belong to
\mathcal{P}
. Inside
B_{2r}({\bm{x}})
, the two processes agree, while green points represent
\check{\mathcal{P}}\setminus B_{2r}({\bm{x}})
. Circles indicate members of the set
\mathcal{Y}
, that is, points connected to
Q\setminus B_{2r}({\bm{x}})
by a path in either
\mathcal{P}
or
\check{\mathcal{P}}
.
The clusters in
\mathcal{P}
intersecting
Q\setminus B_{2r}({\bm{x}})
are shown with black edges, while clusters in
\check{\mathcal{P}}
intersecting
Q\setminus B_{2r}({\bm{x}})
. The dashed black and green edge represents two points that form their own cluster in
\mathcal{P}
but are part of a larger cluster in
\check{\mathcal{P}}
. The blue clusters inside
B_{2r}({\bm{x}})
are common for the two processes. These are thinned in the same order, but different black or green clusters may be visited in between.

Let
\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},\leqslant i}
and
\check{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},\leqslant i}
be the parts constructed before step
i+1
, and
\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},i+1}
and
\check{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},i+1}
be the part of the processes constructed in step
i+1
.
Then,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}}}\neq\check{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}}}\big)\leqslant\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{k=1}^{4\lambda(Q)}\sum_{\check{k}=1}^{4\lambda(Q)}\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},{k+1}}\neq\check{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},\check{k}+1},\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},\leqslant k}=\check{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},\leqslant\check{k}},E_{1,k,\check{k}}\cup E_{2,k,\check{k}}\mid\mathcal{P}_{S_{k}}^{*}\cup\check{\mathcal{P}}^{*}_{\check{S}_{\check{k}}}\big)\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+2\mathbb{P}(E_{\mathsf{dens}})+\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{Y}\cap B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})\neq\varnothing)

\displaystyle\leqslant 16\lambda(Q)^{2}\sup_{k,\check{k}\geqslant 0}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},{k+1}}\neq\check{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},\check{k}+1},\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},\leqslant k}=\check{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},\leqslant\check{k}},E_{1,k,\check{k}}\cup E_{2,k,\check{k}}\mid\mathcal{P}_{S_{k}}^{*}\cup\check{\mathcal{P}}^{*}_{\check{S}_{\check{k}}}\big)\big]

(90)

\displaystyle\quad+2\mathbb{P}(E_{\mathsf{dens}})+\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{Y}\cap B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})\neq\varnothing).

We bound the expectation under each of the two events
E_{1,k,\check{k}}
and
E_{2,k,\check{k}}
separately.

Event
E_{1,k,\check{k}}
.
For
E_{1,k,\check{k}}
, the so far explored points up to step
k
and
\check{k}
, respectively, consists of the union
\mathcal{C}_{k,\check{k}}
of some clusters in
\mathcal{P}_{B_{2r}({\bm{x}})}
that agree for both thinnings (including the current cluster) and some extra points, which we denote by
A_{k}\subseteq\mathcal{P}
and
\check{A}_{\check{k}}\subseteq\check{\mathcal{P}}
, respectively. See Figure
6
for illustration. Note that
\mathcal{C}_{k,\check{k}}\cap\mathcal{Y}=\varnothing
and
A_{k}\cup\check{A}_{\check{k}}\subseteq\mathcal{Y}
. Recall the neighborhood notation
N(B)
from Section
2.1
. The unexplored window before step
k+1
is
Q^{k}=Q_{({\bm{x}}_{0},\infty)}\setminus N(\mathcal{C}_{k-1,\check{k}-1}\cup A_{k})
and the unexplored window before step
\check{k}+1
is
\check{Q}^{\check{k}}=Q_{({\bm{x}}_{0},\infty)}\setminus N(\mathcal{C}_{k-1,\check{k}-1}\cup\check{A}_{\check{k}})
, respectively, where
{\bm{x}}_{0}
is the starting point of the current cluster.

Figure 6.
Illustration of events
E_{1,k,\check{k}}
(left) and
E_{2,k,\check{k}}
(right).
In both panels, black points belong to
\mathcal{P}
, while green points belong to
\check{\mathcal{P}}\setminus\mathcal{P}
.
The orange circle indicates the ball
B_{2r}({\bm{x}})
. Up to step
k
and
\check{k}
, respectively, we have explored the common blue clusters (
\mathcal{C}_{k,\check{k}})
. Additionally, we have explored the black clusters (
A_{k}
) in
\mathcal{P}
and the green clusters (
\check{A}_{\check{k}}
) in
\check{\mathcal{P}}
. Under
E_{1,k,\check{k}}
, we continue exploring the same blue cluster via the dashed path. Under
E_{2,k,\check{k}}
, the blue cluster has been completed and we start a new cluster from the grey point.

Note that
S_{k}\cup\check{S}_{\check{k}}\cup(Q\setminus B_{2r}({\bm{x}}))
is a stopping set (strictly speaking,
S_{k}\times\{1\}\cup\check{S}_{\check{k}}\times\{1\}\cup(Q\setminus B_{2r}({\bm{x}}))\times\{1,2\}
is a stopping set with respect to the Poisson process
\mathcal{P}\times\{1\}\cup\check{\mathcal{P}}_{Q\setminus B_{2r}({\bm{x}})}\times\{2\}
on
Q\times\{1,2\}
). Hence
\mathcal{P}_{B_{2r}({\bm{x}})\setminus(S_{k}\cup\check{S}_{\check{k}})}
is a Poisson process on
B_{2r}({\bm{x}})\setminus({S_{k}}\cup\check{S}_{\check{k}})
conditionally on
\sigma(\mathcal{P}_{S_{k}},\check{\mathcal{P}}_{\check{S}_{\check{k}}})
. We further have
B_{2r}({\bm{x}})\setminus({S_{k}}\cup\check{S}_{\check{k}})=(Q_{({\bm{x}}_{0},\infty)}\cap B_{2r}({\bm{x}}))\setminus N(\mathcal{C}_{k-1,\check{k}-1}\cup A_{k}\cup\check{A}_{\check{k}})
.
Take a Poisson process
\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}
independent of
\sigma(\mathcal{P}_{S_{k}},\check{\mathcal{P}}_{\check{S}_{\check{k}}})
on
N(A_{k}\cup\check{A}_{\check{k}})\cap\big((Q_{({\bm{x}}_{0},\infty)}\cap B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}}))\setminus N(\mathcal{C}_{k-1,\check{k}-1})\big)
and let
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}=\mathcal{P}_{B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})\setminus(S_{k}\cup\check{S}_{\check{k}})}\cup\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}
. Thus,
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
is the union of two independent Poisson processes on the disjoint sets

B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})\setminus(S_{k}\cup\check{S}_{\check{k}})=(Q_{({\bm{x}}_{0},\infty)}\cap B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}}))\setminus N(\mathcal{C}_{k-1,\check{k}-1}\cup A_{k}\cup\check{A}_{\check{k}})

and

N(A_{k}\cup\check{A}_{\check{k}})\cap\big((Q_{({\bm{x}}_{0},\infty)}\cap B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}}))\setminus N(\mathcal{C}_{k-1,\check{k}-1})\big)

whose union is
(Q_{({\bm{x}}_{0},\infty)}\cap B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}}))\setminus N(\mathcal{C}_{k-1,\check{k}-1})
and we may think of
\mathcal{P}_{B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})\setminus(S_{k}\cup\check{S}_{\check{k}})}
as a restriction of
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
.

When a PI on
Q
has the form
\kappa({\bm{w}},\varphi)=\kappa({\bm{w}},\varphi\cap B^{\prime})\mathbbmss{1}_{B}
for some set
B\subseteq B^{\prime}\subseteq Q
, the formula for the retention probabilities shows that

p\big({\bm{w}},Q_{({\bm{w}},\infty)},\phi\big)=p\big({\bm{w}},B_{({\bm{w}},\infty)},\phi\cap B^{\prime}\big)\mathds{1}\{{\bm{w}}\in B\}.

Thus, the Poisson embedding
T_{Q,\psi}(\varphi^{*}_{Q})
equals
T_{B,\psi\cap B^{\prime}}(\varphi^{*}_{B})
.
Taking
B=B^{\prime}=B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})
, this means that the part of the Gibbs process generated in step
{k+1}
by a Poisson embedding on
Q^{k}
equals the Poisson embedding

\mathcal{X}_{k+1}^{\prime\prime\prime}=T_{Q^{k}\cap B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}}),\phi_{k}\cap B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})}\big(\mathcal{P}_{Q^{k}\cap B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})}^{*}\big)\cap S_{k+1},

where
\phi_{k}
are the boundary conditions for this step.
Moreover,
\mathcal{P}_{Q^{k}\cap B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})}\cap S_{k+1}=\mathcal{P}_{B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})\setminus S_{k}}\cap S_{k+1}=\mathcal{P}_{B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})\setminus(S_{k}\cup\check{S}_{\check{k}})}\cap S_{k+1}
since
(\mathcal{P}_{\check{S}_{\check{k}}}\setminus\mathcal{P}_{S_{k}})\cap B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})\subseteq\check{A}_{\check{k}}
and
\mathcal{P}_{S_{k+1}\setminus S_{k}}\cap\check{A}_{\check{k}}=\mathcal{P}_{\check{S}_{\check{k}+1}\setminus\check{S}_{\check{k}}}\cap\check{A}_{\check{k}}=\varnothing
under
E_{1,k,\check{k}}
.
On the event
\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}=\varnothing
, we thus have
\mathcal{P}_{Q^{k}\cap B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})}\cap S_{k+1}=\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\cap S_{k+1}
and hence

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},k+1}

\displaystyle=T_{(Q_{({\bm{x}}_{0},\infty)}\setminus N(\mathcal{C}_{k,\check{k}}))\cap B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}}),\phi_{k}\cap B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})}^{(4)}\big((\mathcal{P}^{\prime})^{*}\big)\cap S_{k+1}.

Here, the thinning
T^{(4)}
used the PI
\kappa^{(4)}_{\bm{x}}({\bm{w}},\varphi)=\kappa^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\bm{x}}({\bm{w}},\varphi)\mathbbmss{1}\big\{{\bm{w}}\in Q^{k}\cap B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})\big\}
.
By similar arguments, if
\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}=\varnothing
, then

\displaystyle\check{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},k+1}={T}_{(Q_{({\bm{x}}_{0},\infty)}\setminus N(\mathcal{C}_{k,\check{k}}))\cap B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}}),\check{\phi}_{\check{k}}\cap B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})}^{(5)}\big((\mathcal{P}^{\prime})^{*}\big)\cap\check{S}_{\check{k}+1},

where the thinning
T^{(5)}
used the PI
\check{\kappa}^{(5)}_{\bm{x}}({\bm{w}},\varphi)=\kappa^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\bm{x}}({\bm{w}},\varphi)\mathbbmss{1}\big\{{\bm{w}}\in\check{Q}^{\check{k}}\cap B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})\big\}
. If the boundary conditions agree, i.e.,
\phi_{k}\cap B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})=\check{\phi}_{\check{k}}\cap B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})
, the only difference is that the PIs
\kappa^{(4)}
and
\kappa^{(5)}
are set to vanish on slightly different sets.
Since

\lambda\big((Q^{k}\cap B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}}))\Delta(\check{Q}^{\check{k}}\cap B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})))\leqslant\lambda(N(A_{k}\cup\check{A}_{\check{k}})\cap B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}}))\leqslant\lambda(N(\mathcal{Y})\cap B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})\big),

taking
P=(Q_{({\bm{x}}_{0},\infty)}\setminus N(\mathcal{C}_{k,\check{k}}))\cap B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})
,
P_{1}=Q^{k}\cap B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})
and
P_{2}=\check{Q}^{\check{k}}\cap B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})
in Lemma
13.1
with
M=4\lambda(Q)
shows

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},k+1}\neq\check{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},\check{k}+1},\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},\leqslant k}=\check{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},\leqslant\check{k}},E_{1,k,\check{k}}\mid\mathcal{P}_{S_{k}}^{*},\check{\mathcal{P}}^{*}_{\check{S}_{\check{k}}}\big)\leqslant\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}\neq\varnothing\mid\mathcal{P}_{S_{k}},\check{\mathcal{P}}_{\check{S}_{\check{k}}}\big)

(91)

\displaystyle\qquad+4\lambda\big(Q\big)\big(2+\lambda(B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}}))\big)\lambda(N(\mathcal{Y})\cap B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}}))+\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{P}(B_{2r}({\bm{x}}))>4\lambda(Q)\mid\mathcal{P}_{S_{k}},\check{\mathcal{P}}_{\check{S}_{\check{k}}}\big).

Note that
\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}\neq\varnothing\mid\mathcal{P}_{S_{k}},\check{\mathcal{P}}_{\check{S}_{\check{k}}}\big)\leqslant\lambda\big(N(A_{k}\cup\check{A}_{\check{k}})\cap B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})\big)
and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\lambda\big(N(\mathcal{Y})\cap B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})\big)\big]\leqslant 2\mathbb{E}\big[\lambda\big(N(\mathcal{P}_{Q\setminus B_{r+4s}({\bm{x}})})\cap B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})\big)\big]+\lambda(Q)\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{Y}\cap B_{r+4s}({\bm{x}})\neq\varnothing)

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{{\bm{w}}\in\mathcal{P}_{Q\setminus B_{r+4s}({\bm{x}})}}\lambda(N({\bm{w}})\cap B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}}))\bigg]+\lambda(Q)\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{Y}\cap B_{r+4s}({\bm{x}})\neq\varnothing)

\displaystyle=2\int_{Q\setminus B_{r+4s}({\bm{x}})}\int_{B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})}\mathbbmss{1}\{{\bm{w}}\sim\bm{z}\}\lambda({\rm d}\bm{z})\lambda({\rm d}{\bm{w}})+\lambda(Q)\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{Y}\cap B_{r+4s}({\bm{x}})\neq\varnothing).

Hence, taking expectations in (
91
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},k+1}\neq\check{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},\check{k}+1},\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},\leqslant k}=\check{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},\leqslant\check{k}},E_{1,k,\check{k}}\mid\mathcal{P}_{S_{k}}^{*},\check{\mathcal{P}}^{*}_{\check{S}_{\check{k}}})\big]

\displaystyle\leqslant C\lambda(Q)^{3}\bigg(\int_{Q\setminus B_{r+4s}({\bm{x}})}\int_{B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})}\mathbbmss{1}\{{\bm{w}}\sim\bm{z}\}\lambda({\rm d}\bm{z})\lambda({\rm d}{\bm{w}})+\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{Y}\cap B_{r+4s}({\bm{x}})\neq\varnothing)\bigg)+\mathbb{P}(E_{\mathsf{dens}}).

By (
10
) and (
11
), this is of order
\lambda(Q)^{-l-2}
if
c_{\mathsf{St}}
is chosen large enough.
Inserting in (
13
) yields the bound under
E_{1,k,\check{k}}
.

Event
E_{2,k,\check{k}}
.
Under
E_{2,k,\check{k}}
, recall that we use the cube-wise ordering introduced in Section
5.2
.

Let
\mathcal{Z}=T_{Q^{k},\varnothing}(\mathcal{P}_{Q^{k}}^{*})
and
\check{\mathcal{Z}}=T_{\check{Q}^{\check{k}},\varnothing}(\check{\mathcal{P}}_{Q^{\check{k}}}^{*})
be the Poisson embeddings in step
k+1
and
\check{k}+1
, respectively. It is enough to check whether
\mathcal{Z}\cap C_{j}\neq\check{\mathcal{Z}}\cap C_{j}
for all cubes
C_{j}
intersecting
B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})
such that
\mathcal{P}\cap C_{p}=\check{\mathcal{P}}\cap C_{p}=\varnothing
for all
p<j
. For such a
j
, we have

\mathcal{Z}\cap C_{j}=T_{Q^{k,j},\phi_{k}}(\mathcal{P}^{*}_{Q^{k,j}})\cap C_{j}=T_{Q_{j}\setminus N(\mathcal{C}_{k,\check{k}}),\phi_{k}}^{(6)}\big(\mathcal{P}^{*}_{Q^{k,j}({\bm{x}})}\big),

where
Q_{j}=Q_{(x_{k},\infty)}\cap B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})\cap(\cup_{p\geqslant j}C_{p})
,
Q^{k,j}=Q^{k}\cap Q_{j}=Q_{j}\setminus N(\mathcal{C}_{k,\check{k}}\cup A_{k})
, and
\kappa^{(6)}_{\bm{x}}({\bm{w}},\varphi)=\kappa^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\bm{x}}({\bm{w}},\varphi)\mathbbmss{1}\big\{{\bm{w}}\in Q^{k,j}\big\}
. Here
Q^{k}
was the unexplored window after step
k
and
Q_{j}
are the unexplored cubes before
C_{j}
. The analogous statement holds for
\check{\mathcal{Z}}\cap C_{j}
. Again,
\mathcal{P}^{*}_{Q^{k,j}\setminus N(\check{A}_{k})}=\check{\mathcal{P}}^{*}_{\check{Q}^{\check{k},j}\setminus N(A_{k})}
is a Poisson process on
Q_{j}\setminus N(\mathcal{C}_{k,\check{k}}\cup A_{k}\cup A_{\check{k}})=Q^{k,j}\setminus N(\check{A}_{k})=\check{Q}^{\check{k},j}\setminus N(A_{k})
given
\mathcal{P}_{S_{k}\cup(\cup_{p<j}C_{p})}
and
\check{\mathcal{P}}_{\check{S}_{\check{k}}\cup(\cup_{p<j}C_{p})}
. Hence, we may argue as under
E_{1,k,\check{k}}
to see that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},k+1}\cap C_{j}\neq\check{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},\check{k}+1}\cap C_{j},\mathcal{P}\cap C_{p}=\check{\mathcal{P}}\cap C_{p}=\varnothing,p<j,\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},\leqslant k}=\check{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},\leqslant\check{k}},

\displaystyle\qquad E_{2,k,\check{k}}\mid\mathcal{P}^{*}_{S_{k}\cup(\cup_{p<j}{C_{p}})},\check{\mathcal{P}}^{*}_{\check{S}_{\check{k}}\cup(\cup_{p<j}{C_{p}})}\big)\big]

\displaystyle\leqslant C\lambda(Q)^{3}\bigg(\int_{Q\setminus B_{r+4s}({\bm{x}})}\int_{B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})}\mathbbmss{1}\{\bm{z}\sim{\bm{w}}\}\lambda({\rm d}\bm{z})\lambda({\rm d}{\bm{w}})+\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{Y}\cap B_{r+4s}({\bm{x}})\neq\varnothing)\bigg)+\mathbb{P}(E_{\mathsf{dens}}).

The number of cubes intersecting
B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})
is of order
\lambda(B_{r+3s+1}({\bm{x}}))\leqslant\lambda(Q)
, which leads to the bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},k+1}\neq\check{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},\check{k}+1},\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},\leqslant k}\neq\check{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},\leqslant\check{k}},E_{2,k,\check{k}}\big)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\lambda(Q)^{4}\bigg(\int_{Q\setminus B_{r+4s}({\bm{x}})}\int_{B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})}\mathbbmss{1}\{{\bm{w}}\sim\bm{z}\}\lambda({\rm d}\bm{z})\lambda({\rm d}{\bm{w}})+\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{Y}\cap B_{r+4s}({\bm{x}})\neq\varnothing)+\mathbb{P}(E_{\mathsf{dens}})\bigg).

This is again of order
\lambda(Q)^{-l-2}
when
c_{\mathsf{St}}
is large enough.

Model (P)
For Model (P), we let
\Gamma=\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{cl}}(\Phi_{Q,\varnothing}^{*})
and
\check{\Gamma}=\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{cl}}(\check{\Phi}_{Q,\varnothing}^{*})
and define

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}_{0}

\displaystyle=\big\{{\bm{w}}\in\mathcal{P}\cup\check{\mathcal{P}}\mid{\bm{w}}\leftrightsquigarrow_{\Gamma}\mathcal{P}_{Q\setminus B_{2r}({\bm{x}})}\text{ or }{\bm{w}}\leftrightsquigarrow_{\check{\Gamma}}\check{\mathcal{P}}_{Q\setminus B_{2r}({\bm{x}})}\big\}

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}

\displaystyle=\big\{{\bm{w}}\in\mathcal{P}\cup\check{\mathcal{P}}\mid{\bm{w}}\leftrightsquigarrow_{\Gamma}\mathcal{Y}_{0}\cap\mathcal{P}\text{ or }{\bm{w}}\leftrightsquigarrow_{\check{\Gamma}}\mathcal{Y}_{0}\cap\check{\mathcal{P}}\big\}.

Here we construct
\mathcal{Y}
in two steps to ensure that it is a union of clusters in both
\Gamma
and
\check{\Gamma}
. This is has to do with the exploration based construction of the RCMs, which makes it possible to have an edge between two points in
\mathcal{P}_{B_{2r}({\bm{x}})}
in
\Gamma
but not in
\check{\Gamma}
. This was not possible when the graphs were based on a symmetric relation.
Note that
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{Y}\cap B_{r+4s}({\bm{x}})\neq\varnothing)\leqslant\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{Y}_{0}\cap B_{r+4.5s}({\bm{x}})\neq\varnothing)+2\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{P}_{B_{r+4s}({\bm{x}})}\leftrightsquigarrow_{\Gamma}\mathcal{P}_{Q\setminus B_{r+4.5s}({\bm{x}})}),

which we may bound using the assumption (
16
).

The two graphs
\Gamma
and
\check{\Gamma}
have the same clusters on
\mathcal{P}\setminus\mathcal{Y}=\check{\mathcal{P}}\setminus\mathcal{Y}
, and the points are visited in the same order. To see this, consider the construction of
\Gamma
and
\check{\Gamma}
as thinnings of directed graphs
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}
and
\overrightarrow{\check{\Gamma}}
from Remark
12.3
. Recall that a cluster in
\Gamma
starts at a point
{\bm{x}}_{0}\in\mathcal{P}
that has no directed path in
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}
to an
\iota
-smaller point. If
{\bm{x}}_{0}\notin\mathcal{Y}
, there can be no directed path from
{\bm{x}}_{0}
to a smaller point in
\overrightarrow{\check{\Gamma}}
either since this would have to pass through
\check{\mathcal{P}}_{Q\setminus B_{2r}({\bm{x}})}
and hence
{\bm{x}}_{0}
would be in
\mathcal{Y}_{0}
. Thus,
{\bm{x}}_{0}
must also be a starting point of a cluster in
\check{\Gamma}
. If a point
\bm{z}
is in the cluster of
{\bm{x}}_{0}
in
\Gamma
, then there is a directed path from
\bm{z}
to
{\bm{x}}_{0}
in
\overrightarrow{\Gamma}
, but not to any smaller point. This path cannot go via any points of
\mathcal{Y}_{0}
, otherwise
{\bm{x}}_{0}
would be in
\mathcal{Y}
. Hence, the path would also be a path in
\overrightarrow{\check{\Gamma}}
. There cannot be any path from
\bm{z}
to a smaller point than
{\bm{x}}_{0}
in
\overrightarrow{\check{\Gamma}}
since such a path would have to go via
\check{\mathcal{P}}_{Q\setminus B_{2r}({\bm{x}})}\subseteq\mathcal{Y}_{0}
, which would again imply
\bm{z}\in\mathcal{Y}_{0}
and hence
{\bm{x}}_{0}\in\mathcal{Y}
. Thus, the clusters starting at an
{\bm{x}}_{0}\notin\mathcal{Y}
agree, and they are explored in the same order. In particular, at each steps
k,\check{k}
in the two explorations, the clusters of
\mathcal{G}^{\mathsf{cl}}_{k}
and
\check{\mathcal{G}}_{\check{k}}^{\mathsf{cl}}
consist of a union of clusters
\mathcal{C}_{k,\check{k}}
that agree (including the one being explored, if it agrees so far) and some extra points
A_{k}\subseteq\mathcal{P}
and
\check{A}_{\check{k}}\subseteq\check{\mathcal{P}}
, just as under Model (L).

We define events
E_{1,k,\check{k}}
and
E_{2,k,\check{k}}
as for Model (L). Then, (
13
) holds again, where the expectation for given
k,\check{k}
can be computed as

\lim_{\delta\to 0}\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{X}^{\delta,\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},{k+1}}\neq\check{\mathcal{X}}^{\delta,\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},\check{k}+1},\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},\leqslant k}=\check{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},\leqslant\check{k}},E_{1,k,\check{k}}\cup E_{2,k,\check{k}}\mid\Phi_{Q,\varnothing}^{*}\cap S_{k},\check{\Phi}^{*}_{Q,\varnothing}\cap\check{S}_{\check{k}})].

Here
\mathcal{X}^{\delta,\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},{k+1}}
and
\check{\mathcal{X}}^{\delta,\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},\check{k}+1}
are the processes generated on
Q^{\delta}
in step
k+1
and
\check{k}+1
, respectively, and
\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},\leqslant k}
and
\check{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}},\leqslant\check{k}}
are the parts of
\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}}}
and
\check{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\bm{x}}}
generated before step
k+1
and
\check{k}+1
.

Inductive application of Lemma
6.4
shows that conditionally on
\Phi_{Q,\varnothing}^{*}\cap S_{k}
, we have that
\bar{B}^{*}_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})\cap(\Phi_{Q,\varnothing}^{*}\setminus\bar{S}_{k}^{*})
is a Poisson process of intensity

\rho_{k}(\bm{z})=\mathbbmss{1}\big\{\bm{z}\in Q_{({\bm{x}}_{k},\infty)}\cap B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})\big\}\prod_{{\bm{y}}\in S_{k-1}}(1-\pi(\bm{z},{\bm{y}})).

Moreover, conditionally on
\Phi_{Q,\varnothing}^{*}\cap\bar{S}_{k}^{*}
and
\check{\Phi}_{Q,\varnothing}^{*}\cap\bar{\check{S}}_{\check{k}}^{*}
, we have that
\bar{B}^{*}_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})\cap\big(\Phi_{Q,\varnothing}^{*}\setminus(\bar{S}_{k}^{*}\cup\bar{\check{S}}^{*}_{\check{k}})\big)
is a Poisson process of reduced intensity
\rho_{k,\check{k}}\lambda
, where

\rho_{k,\check{k}}(\bm{z})=\mathbbmss{1}\big\{\bm{z}\in Q_{({\bm{x}}_{k},\infty)}\cap Q_{(\check{\bm{x}}_{\check{k}},\infty)}\cap B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})\big\}\prod_{{\bm{y}}\in S_{k-1}\cup\check{S}_{\check{k}-1}}(1-\pi(\bm{z},{\bm{y}})).

It follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\bar{B}^{*}_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})\cap(\Phi_{Q,\varnothing}^{*}\setminus\bar{S}_{k}^{*})\cap\bar{\check{S}}^{*}_{\check{k}}\neq\varnothing,E_{1,k,\check{k}}\cup E_{2,k,\check{k}})

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathbb{E}\big[\big(\big|\bar{B}^{*}_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})\cap(\Phi_{Q,\varnothing}^{*}\setminus\bar{S}_{k}^{*})\big|-\big|\bar{B}^{*}_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})\cap(\Phi_{Q,\varnothing}^{*}\setminus(\bar{S}_{k}^{*}\cup\bar{\check{S}}^{*}_{\check{k}}))\big|\big)\mathbbmss{1}\big\{E_{1,k,\check{k}}\cup E_{2,k,\check{k}}\big\}\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg(\int_{B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})}\rho_{k}(\bm{z}){\rm d}\bm{z}-\int_{B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})}\rho_{k,\check{k}}(\bm{z}){\rm d}\bm{z}\bigg)\mathbbmss{1}\big\{E_{1,k,\check{k}}\cup E_{2,k,\check{k}}\big\}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})}\bigg(1-\prod_{{\bm{y}}\in\check{A}_{\check{k}}}(1-\pi(\bm{z},{\bm{y}}))\bigg){\rm d}\bm{z}\mathbbmss{1}\big\{E_{1,k,\check{k}}\cup E_{2,k,\check{k}}\big\}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbbmss{1}\big\{\mathcal{Y}\cap B_{r+4s}({\bm{x}})=\varnothing,\check{A}_{\check{k}}\subseteq\mathcal{Y}\big\}\sum_{{\bm{y}}\in\check{A}_{\check{k}}}\int_{B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})}\pi(\bm{z},{\bm{y}}){\rm d}\bm{z}\bigg]+\lambda(B_{r+4s}({\bm{x}}))\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{Y}\cap B_{r+4s}({\bm{x}})\neq\varnothing)

(92)

\displaystyle=2\int_{Q\setminus B_{r+4s}({\bm{x}})}\int_{B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})}\pi(\bm{z},{\bm{y}}){\rm d}\bm{z}{\rm d}{\bm{y}}+\lambda(B_{r+4s}({\bm{x}}))\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{Y}\cap B_{r+4s}({\bm{x}})\neq\varnothing).

Then,
\mathbb{P}(\bar{B}^{*}_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})\cap(\Phi_{Q,\varnothing}^{*}\setminus\bar{S}_{k}^{*})\neq\bar{B}^{*}_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})\cap(\check{\Phi}_{Q,\varnothing}^{*}\setminus\bar{\check{S}}^{*}_{\check{k}}))
is bounded by twice the bound in (
92
).

We may extend the Poisson process
\mathcal{P}_{B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})}\setminus(S_{k}\cup\check{S}_{\check{k}})
to a Poisson process
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
of intensity
\rho_{k,\check{k}}(\bm{z})=\prod_{{\bm{y}}\in\mathcal{C}_{k,\check{k}}}(1-\pi(\bm{z},{\bm{y}}))
on
B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})
and attach arbitrary marks to the additional points to form the process
\Phi^{\prime}
. We can bound the probability
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\neq\mathcal{P}_{B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})}\setminus(S_{k}\cup\check{S}_{\check{k}})
by computing as in (
92
). Thus we may assume that we are thinning
\Phi^{\prime}
. Lifting
\Phi^{\prime}
to
Q^{\delta}
and arguing as in Model (L), we find that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{X}_{{\bm{x}},k+1}^{\delta,\prime\prime\prime}\neq\check{\mathcal{X}}_{{\bm{x}},\check{k}+1}^{\delta,\prime\prime\prime},\mathcal{X}_{{\bm{x}},\leqslant k}^{\delta,\prime\prime\prime}=\check{\mathcal{X}}_{{\bm{x}},\leqslant\check{k}}^{\delta,\prime\prime\prime},E_{1,k,\check{k}}\cup E_{2,k,\check{k}})\leqslant C\lambda(Q)^{4}\bigg(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{Y}\cap B_{r+4s}({\bm{x}})\neq\varnothing)+\mathbb{P}(E_{\mathsf{dens}})+\mathbb{P}(E^{\delta})

\displaystyle+\int_{Q\setminus B_{r+4s}({\bm{x}})}\int_{B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})}\pi({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}}){\rm d}{\bm{x}}{\rm d}{\bm{y}}

\displaystyle+\int_{B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})\times\mathbb{U}^{\delta}}\int_{Q^{\delta}\setminus(B_{r+4s}({\bm{x}})\times\mathbb{U}^{\delta})}\mathbbmss{1}\{(\bm{z},{\bm{r}})\approx^{\delta}({\bm{y}},{\bm{s}})\}(\lambda\times\mathsf{Leb}_{[0,1]}^{\delta})^{2}({\rm d}({\bm{y}},{\bm{s}},\bm{z},{\bm{r}}))\bigg)

where
\approx^{\delta}
is the symmetric relation corresponding to
\pi
, see Section
6.3
.
Integrating over
\mathbb{U}^{\delta}\times\mathbb{U}^{\delta}
and letting
\delta\to\infty
, the second integral converges to

\int_{B_{r+3s}({\bm{x}})}\int_{Q\setminus B_{r+4s}({\bm{x}})}\pi(\bm{z},{\bm{w}})\lambda({\rm d}{\bm{y}})\lambda({\rm d}\bm{z}).

The claim thus follows from the assumptions (
16
) and (
17
).
∎
